| F2 | **REFINE** — The pin checks rounded nominal `RATE × ACCRUE_S`; it does not independently pin rate, duration, or delivered ticks. |
| F3 | **REFINE** — The zero-count defect is caught, but counting two `[DX]` records does not require one D and one X. |
| F4 | **UPHOLD** — The explicit exclusion of additional probe-logic edits establishes a defensible boundary for these integrity checks. |
| F5 | **REFINE** — R6b closes the demonstrated fixed-index mistakes; the broader timer-label closure claim exceeds its coverage of re-arm states. |
| F6 | **REFINE** — R6b catches `M_pct3`, but it does not supply per-timer rate equality across R5’s other configurations. |
| F7 | **UPHOLD** — Under the cited enumeration, adding a count predicate to the unconditional store introduces an additional partition; filing is consistent. |
| F8 | **REFINE** — The measured edits are detected, but `grep -cF` pins substrings, not whole named, successful rows. |
| F9 | **UPHOLD** — Separating the observation mechanisms fixes the original overgeneralisation. |
| F10 | **UPHOLD** — Reset-to-zero can produce a dead-clock observation at multiples of 763, including 3052. |
| F11 | **UPHOLD** — The replacement accurately limits what the two starvation measurements establish. |
| F12 | **UPHOLD** — The ENABLE claim now carries the unchecked-disable assumption, while remaining mode coverage is disclosed. |
| F13 | **UPHOLD** — Setting a mode during register tests does not establish callback coverage under that mode. |
| F14 | **OVERTURN** — The revised probe header still says gate 2 has “every arm of it enabled”; that remains false of `diff_footbridge.c`. |
| F15 | **UPHOLD** — The added starting-state, acknowledgment and delivered-count qualifications support the arithmetic. |
| F16 | **UPHOLD** — The no-ack qualification correctly limits the equality-comparison argument. |
| F17 | **UPHOLD** — Including R2 corrects the cited detector coverage. |
| F18 | **REFINE** — Pre-fix `X = D = 3051` also assumes the disable executed; equality of those results does not prove disabled accrual. |
| F19 | **UPHOLD** — The revised header distinguishes instruction-word checking from the absent branch-disassembly comparison. |
| F20 | **UPHOLD** — “Only GATED” is the appropriately limited claim. |
| F21 | **UPHOLD** — Removing “smallest,” identifying the prescaler setting, and correcting the overflow claim are justified. |
| F22 | **UPHOLD** — `INT_MAX / 68,157,440 ≈ 31.5077 s`; the corrected denominator explains the number. |
| F23 | **UPHOLD** — The local correction properly credits R5 and withdraws the erroneous R2 kill. |
| F24 | **OVERTURN** — The control-flow correction is right; the addendum’s replacement assertion that internal budgets bound completion is false. |
| F25 | **UPHOLD** — The reported mutant/baseline distinction and substantive-failure/aggregate distinction are appropriate. |
| F26 | **UPHOLD** — “That count” identifies the quantity actually compared with 762. |
| F27 | **UPHOLD** — The reported slowest-rate R5 failure and revision-3 end-count pins justify withdrawing the reset-to-two debt. |
| F28 | **REFINE** — Killing `M_lowonly` on initial allocation does not establish coverage of an upward re-arm after successful initialization. |
| F29 | **UPHOLD** — The precharge is observed through `owed2`; unused returned extrema are harmless, and format changes intentionally fail the pins. |
| F30 | **OVERTURN** — Divergence already exists in the reviewed files; this is an actual wrong-record problem, not merely a future maintenance risk. |
| F31 | **OVERTURN** — Sustained output can keep `Session.wait()` running indefinitely; the claimed internal backstop does not exist. |
| F32 | **UPHOLD** — The end-count observations close the measured retargeting hole. The cap concerns further harness edits, not product-coverage findings. |
| D1 | **UPHOLD** — A measured four-timer claim warrants checks that distinguish four timers from four executions. |
| D2 | **UPHOLD** — The corrected explanation identifies the delivery ceiling needed for the arithmetic. |
| D3 | **UPHOLD** — The existing rule supports filing invented store predicates; it does not require their newly introduced partitions. |
| D4 | **REFINE** — Distinct-rate R6b and per-timer R5 checks cover different failures; neither generally subsumes the other. |

For question 2, **eight accepted dispositions cannot stand unchanged: F2, F3, F5, F6, F8, F14, F18 and F24.** Two warrant overturning as written; six need narrower claims or acknowledgment of incomplete coverage. F28, F30 and F31 are additional corrections to rejected/noted dispositions and are excluded from that count.

For question 3, these are the missed distinctions and remaining holes:

1. **Timer labels and re-arm states have not been closed together.** Only timer 2 receives the above-new-bound re-arm in [R6](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c:299). R6b starts every counter at zero.

   A timer-1-only version of the supplied `M_eq` is therefore a concrete **unmeasured counterexample candidate**: R6 never exercises timer 1, and the ordinary zero-start sweeps cannot distinguish equality from greater-or-equal. The packet already reports that the drain misses the unrestricted `M_eq`.

   The measurement owed is that label-specific case against revision 3, including its pins. Source analysis predicts the reviewed property checks will pass; I am **not** reporting a measured battery escape. This concerns the existing callback predicate and its timer-label axis, so F7’s unconditional-store ruling does not dispose of it.

2. **The replacement timeout explanation is demonstrably wrong.** [Session.wait()](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:332) resets its deadline whenever the buffer grows. An outer drain deadline cannot interrupt a call stuck inside it, and `close()` has not yet been reached.

   I exercised the actual method in isolation with a fake clock: the silent control returned at 20 simulated seconds; the output-producing fixture continued through 120 seconds and returned only when EOF was supplied at second 121. Correct the [addendum’s bounded-completion claim](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbdrainwire_20260924/PRETABLE_ADDENDUM_r2.out:20). Any decision to retain the missing watchdog must acknowledge potentially unbounded waiting.

3. **R6b does not repair R5’s aggregation across its entire rate matrix.** [R5](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c:220) still accepts the maximum peak. An incorrectly lowered bound affecting one timer only in a high-rate case can be hidden by another timer’s correct peak, while all four low-rate R6b cases remain correct. That is why D4’s “stronger” argument is insufficient. Either assert equality per timer in those cases or explicitly retain that coverage limitation.

4. **Initial arming is not upward re-arming.** `M_lowonly` fails because calloc supplies zero. A different case—initialization succeeds, subsequent bound increases are omitted—is not settled by that measurement. The missing measurement is an upward re-arm from an already established positive bound. Under the store ruling, this can remain disclosed debt; F28 should not present its rejection as closing the direction gap.

5. **The new backlog row pins quantity without identity.** The [combined pattern](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_arm.sh:1246) accepts both these record sets:

   ```text
   D=3, X=3
   D=3, D=3
   ```

   An isolated evaluation of the actual pattern produced two matches for each. This demonstrates the range row’s identity gap, not a product failure or a full-probe escape. File it under the spent harness-edit allowance. Likewise, describe F8’s checks accurately as payload-substring pins.

6. **Several corrections leave contradictory assertions nearby.** The detector header still says removing the ceiling [causes overflow](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c:18); R3 still says the drain [cannot distinguish reset-to-zero at all](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c:231); the assembler comment still claims [every generated word is checked](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:156). These are precisely the claims this round corrects elsewhere.

   The newly edited header also retains both the [pre-fix disable “proof”](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:55) and the [gate-2 enabled-arms overstatement](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:62). F30 cannot remain “noted, no change.”

Before commit, resolve the label-specific re-arm coverage finding, correct the surviving false claims, explicitly record the remaining limitations, and obtain the owed clean gate-14 run on the final revision. The additional harness-pin gaps can remain filed under the stated cap.

5. **The new backlog row pins quantity without identity.** The [combined pattern](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_arm.sh:1246) accepts both these record sets:

   ```text
   D=3, X=3
   D=3, D=3
   ```

   An isolated evaluation of the actual pattern produced two matches for each. This demonstrates the range row’s identity gap, not a product failure or a full-probe escape. File it under the spent harness-edit allowance. Likewise, describe F8’s checks accurately as payload-substring pins.

6. **Several corrections leave contradictory assertions nearby.** The detector header still says removing the ceiling [causes overflow](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c:18); R3 still says the drain [cannot distinguish reset-to-zero at all](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c:231); the assembler comment still claims [every generated word is checked](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:156). These are precisely the claims this round corrects elsewhere.

   The newly edited header also retains both the [pre-fix disable “proof”](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:55) and the [gate-2 enabled-arms overstatement](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:62). F30 cannot remain “noted, no change.”

Before commit, resolve the label-specific re-arm coverage finding, correct the surviving false claims, explicitly record the remaining limitations, and obtain the owed clean gate-14 run on the final revision. The additional harness-pin gaps can remain filed under the stated cap.

GO-WITH-CHANGES
